Like before, maybe I’d have a bad day here or there, but this has felt more steady for these past couple of weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other times in your life when you felt this kind of low mood and loss of interest in things, lasting at least a couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Looking back, it hasn’t just been these two weeks out of nowhere. I’ve had these low stretches coming and going for maybe about a year now. They’d sort of lift and then come back, and I’d tell myself, “Well, I’m just tired,” or “Work is a lot,” or “That’s just how life feels right now.”\n\nBut this latest one feels more… connected to my husband stopping work and being home after retiring. That seemed to change something in the house, in the routine, and then this low feeling settled in more clearly. So yes, I’ve had other times like it over the past year, on and off, but this most recent spell feels tied to that change.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had a period where your mood was low and you lost interest in things for more than just a few days—maybe affecting your work or home life—where you felt like you needed help or it was really noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Before this past year… I’m not really sure I can point to anything like that, no. I mean, I’ve had hard times like anybody—days where I was tired of everything, or didn’t feel like going out, or felt weighed down—but I don’t know that I ever had a stretch before this where it lasted and really changed how I was at work or at home in a way people would’ve said, “Something is wrong with her.”\n\nI’ve always kind of pushed through things, especially with work and family. So maybe I wouldn’t have named it even if it was happening, but sitting here trying to think back, nothing stands out to me before this past year the way these recent spells do. This year is the first time it’s felt noticeable enough that I’m talking about it like this.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any of those low stretches over the past year—before your husband retired—where your mood was low and you lost interest in things? Did any of those last for more than two weeks and feel like they were affecting your work or home life at the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think some of them did go longer than just a few days. It’s hard for me to measure it exactly, because when you’re in it, the days kind of run together, and I was still doing what I had to do. But over this past year, before my husband retired, I would have these stretches where I felt low and just not interested in much, and they’d come and go.\n\nI’d say some probably lasted a couple of weeks or more, but this latest one is the one that stands out the most because it feels more tied to him stopping work and suddenly being home all the time. That change made it more noticeable, like the whole routine of the house shifted and my mood seemed to sink with it.\n\nBefore that, it was more on and off, and I kept telling myself it was just a phase or being tired. It may have affected home and work some, but I think I was trying to push through and not make much of it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time in the past year, before your husband retired, when you felt low and uninterested in things for more than two weeks, even if it wasn’t connected to any specific event or change in routine?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so… I mean, it’s hard for me to pull out one exact time and say, “That was the start and that was the end,” because it wasn’t like a switch flipped. It was more like these low stretches kept coming back over the past year, sort of rolling in and then easing up, and then after a while I’d realize I’d been feeling that way for weeks.\n\nBefore my husband retired, there were definitely times where I just felt down and not interested in much of anything, and I don’t think they were always tied to one clear event. I’d try to explain it away as being tired or just having a lot on my mind, but looking back, yes, some of those spells were probably more than two weeks.\n\nThe difference is that this latest one feels more obvious to me because it seemed to happen after my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That change made it stand out more, I guess. But the low periods themselves had already been coming and going for about a year before that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had multiple times when you felt down or lost interest in things, with each episode lasting at least two weeks, and with not more than a few weeks in between where you felt completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years… I don’t know if I can say it goes back the full two years in that pattern. What I can say is that for about the last year, these down stretches have been coming back on and off. I’d feel low and not really interested in things for a while, then it would ease up some, and then later it would come back again.\n\nI’m not sure I was ever completely “back to normal” in between, but there were times I felt more like myself and could just keep going, so I kind of told myself it had passed. It’s hard for me to mark it out neatly, like two weeks here and three weeks there, because I wasn’t writing it down or anything.\n\nBut yes, in this past year it does feel like there have been repeated stretches like that. This latest one just feels more obvious to me, and more connected to after my husband stopped working and was suddenly home all the time. That change made it stand out more than the earlier ones.\n\nDoctor: Between those low periods you described over the past year, did you ever feel completely back to your usual self for a while before the next one started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I ever felt completely back to my old self, not all the way. There were times in between where I was better, like I could get through the day more normally and I wasn’t feeling quite as weighed down, so I’d tell myself, “Okay, maybe that’s over now.” But looking back, I’m not sure it really lifted completely.\n\nIt’s more like the low periods have been coming and going for about a year. Some weeks would be better than others, and then it would sort of creep back in again. This most recent stretch just feels clearer to me because it seemed to get worse after my husband stopped working and was around the house all the time. That change made it stand out more, but the ups and downs were already there before that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had multiple times when you felt down or lost interest in things, with each episode lasting at least two weeks and not feeling completely like yourself in between?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say, at least for about the last year. I don’t know that I can stretch it neatly over the whole two years, because I’m not always good at remembering it in clean blocks like that, but over this past year the depressed stretches have definitely been coming back on and off.\n\nIt wasn’t like I’d feel bad for a day or two and then be fine. It would sort of settle in for a while, and then ease up, and I’d think, “Well, maybe I’m better now,” but I don’t know that I ever felt completely like myself in between. Maybe better, yes, able to function more normally, but not fully back to how I used to feel.\n\nAnd then this latest stretch feels more noticeable to me because it seemed tied to after my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That change made it feel more obvious, like I could point to something and say, “This got worse around then.” But the low periods themselves had already been coming and going before that, for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two separate times in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks each, with at least some time in between where you felt noticeably better, even if not completely like yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’d say there have been separate times, at least over this past year. I’m not sure I can lay them out perfectly, like, “this one started here and ended there,” because I wasn’t tracking it that way, but the low stretches have been coming back on and off.\n\nThere would be a period where I felt down or just not very interested in things, and it wasn’t just a day or two. It would hang around long enough that I noticed it. Then it would ease up some, and I’d feel better for a while — not exactly completely like myself, but better enough that I could sort of tell myself maybe I was coming out of it.\n\nThis latest one feels more clear to me because it got worse after my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That’s the one I can point to more easily. But yes, before that, there had already been these depressed stretches coming and going for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past year, can you identify at least two separate times when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks each, where the low periods were clearly separated by at least a few weeks of feeling better — even if not completely like yourself — before another low period started?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a general way, but I have a hard time saying it was clearly separated, like with exact weeks marked off. Over the past year, the down times have been coming back on and off. There would be a stretch where I felt low or just not interested in much, and it would last longer than just a few days. Then it would ease up for a while, and I’d feel better — maybe not back to my usual self, but better enough that I could notice the difference.\n\nSo I do think there were at least a couple of those periods. It’s just that they kind of blur together when I look back, because I wasn’t writing it down or anything. The latest one stands out more because it seemed to get worse after my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That’s the one I can point to more clearly. But before that, yes, there had already been these depressed stretches coming and going for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you say for sure that there were at least two separate times in the past year when you felt down or uninterested in things for at least two weeks each, with a clear period of improvement in between—where you felt noticeably better, even if not completely like your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “for sure” in the very clean way you’re asking, with the weeks separated out clearly. I wish I could, but when I look back on the year it all kind of runs together in my head.\n\nWhat I can say is the depressed stretches have been coming back on and off for about a year. It hasn’t felt like just one bad day here or there. It’s more like I’d go through a low spell, then it would lift some, and I’d think maybe I was doing better, and then at some point it would come back again.\n\nThe latest one is the easiest for me to point to, because it really seemed to get worse after my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That change made it stand out more to me, like I could connect it to something. But before that, there were already these ups and downs with feeling low over the past year. I just can’t swear to the exact number of weeks or how clear the break was in between.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past two years, were there at least two separate times when you felt down or uninterested in things for at least two weeks, and in between those times, you had at least a couple of months where you felt mostly back to normal — not perfect, but close enough that you didn’t feel like you were struggling most days?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years… I don’t know that I can honestly say it that way, with a couple of months where I felt mostly normal in between. I can look back more clearly over about the past year, and what I notice is these depressed stretches have been coming and going on and off.\n\nIt’s not like I can point to two neat episodes and then say, “Yes, then I had two good months where I wasn’t really struggling.” It feels messier than that. That’s when I really noticed it more. Before that, there were still low periods, but I’m not sure I can say there were two separate ones with months of feeling close to normal in between.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past year, was there ever a time when you felt completely back to normal for at least two months before another low period started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say I was completely back to normal for two full months, no. That’s the part that’s hard for me, because there were times when I felt better, like the heaviness wasn’t as bad and I could get through things more easily, but I don’t remember thinking, “Okay, I’m really myself again,” for that long.\n\nOver the past year it’s felt more like these low stretches keep coming and going. I know I’m probably repeating myself, but I really don’t think I can divide it up that neatly. Over about the past year, the low stretches have been coming back on and off. They’ll ease up some, and I’ll think maybe I’m doing better, but then it seems to come around again.

It’s hard for me to mark it out neatly, like two weeks here and three weeks there, because I wasn’t writing it down or anything.\n\nBut yes, in this past year it does feel like there have been repeated stretches like that. This latest one just feels more obvious to me, and more connected to after my husband stopped working and was suddenly home all the time. That change made it stand out more than the earlier ones.\n\nDoctor: Between those low periods you described over the past year, did you ever feel completely back to your usual self for a while before the next one started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I ever felt completely back to my old self, not all the way. Some weeks would be better than others, and then it would sort of creep back in again. This most recent stretch just feels clearer to me because it seemed to get worse after my husband stopped working and was around the house all the time. That change made it stand out more, but the ups and downs were already there before that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had multiple times when you felt down or lost interest in things, with each episode lasting at least two weeks and not feeling completely like yourself in between?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say, at least for about the last year. I don’t know that I can stretch it neatly over the whole two years, because I’m not always good at remembering it in clean blocks like that, but over this past year the depressed stretches have definitely been coming back on and off.\n\nIt wasn’t like I’d feel bad for a day or two and then be fine. It would sort of settle in for a while, and then ease up, and I’d think, “Well, maybe I’m better now,” but I don’t know that I ever felt completely like myself in between. Maybe better, yes, able to function more normally, but not fully back to how I used to feel.\n\nAnd then this latest stretch feels more noticeable to me because it seemed tied to after my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That change made it feel more obvious, like I could point to something and say, “This got worse around then.” But the low periods themselves had already been coming and going before that, for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two separate times in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks each, with at least some time in between where you felt noticeably better, even if not completely like yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’d say there have been separate times, at least over this past year. I’m not sure I can lay them out perfectly, like, “this one started here and ended there,” because I wasn’t tracking it that way, but the low stretches have been coming back on and off.\n\nThere would be a period where I felt down or just not very interested in things, and it wasn’t just a day or two. It would hang around long enough that I noticed it. Then it would ease up some, and I’d feel better for a while — not exactly completely like myself, but better enough that I could sort of tell myself maybe I was coming out of it.\n\nThis latest one feels more clear to me because it got worse after my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That’s the one I can point to more easily. But yes, before that, there had already been these depressed stretches coming and going for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past year, can you identify at least two separate times when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks each, where the low periods were clearly separated by at least a few weeks of feeling better — even if not completely like yourself — before another low period started?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a general way, but I have a hard time saying it was clearly separated, like with exact weeks marked off. Over the past year, the down times have been coming back on and off. There would be a stretch where I felt low or just not interested in much, and it would last longer than just a few days. Then it would ease up for a while, and I’d feel better — maybe not back to my usual self, but better enough that I could notice the difference.\n\nSo I do think there were at least a couple of those periods. But before that, yes, there had already been these depressed stretches coming and going for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you say for sure that there were at least two separate times in the past year when you felt down or uninterested in things for at least two weeks each, with a clear period of improvement in between—where you felt noticeably better, even if not completely like your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “for sure” in the very clean way you’re asking, with the weeks separated out clearly. I can look back more clearly over about the past year, and what I notice is these depressed stretches have been coming and going on and off.\n\nIt’s not like I can point to two neat episodes and then say, “Yes, then I had two good months where I wasn’t really struggling.” It feels messier than that. There would be times where I wasn’t as low, and I could function better, and I’d think maybe it was lifting. Before that, there were still low periods, but I’m not sure I can say there were two separate ones with months of feeling close to normal in between.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past year, was there ever a time when you felt completely back to normal for at least two months before another low period started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say I was completely back to normal for two full months, no. Right, I understand what you’re asking. I still don’t think I can say there was that kind of clear break, like two whole months where I wasn’t struggling most days and felt completely like myself again.

The best way I can describe it is that the down periods have been coming and going for about a year. Sometimes it lets up a bit, and I can look back and think, well, maybe I was doing a little better then, but it doesn’t feel like I had this solid stretch where everything was back to normal.

And this latest low period feels different in my mind because it seems more tied to when my husband stopped working and was home all the time. That’s when it stood out more to me, or when it felt like it got heavier. So I can say it’s been on and off, but I can’t really say there were two separate times with two normal months in between.
